I hear Garrett’s voice clearly, like he’s right behind me—on the trail or in my painting room—and in that moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes, I can control what I’m doing, but it gets really hard to stay grounded. When I hear Garrett’s voice, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in the moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that. I’ve had hard things happen, but I don’t know if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s stressful because it sounds so real, like Garrett is right behind me. Even though I know he’s dead, when I hear it on the trail or in my painting room, it feels completely real in that moment, and that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt overwhelmed or kind of unreal at times, but I don’t remember feeling like I was actually outside my body watching myself.\n\nDoctor: Was Garrett's death a sudden or unexpected event for you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t have a clear answer for that. I know his death affects me, but whether it was sudden or unexpected… I don’t know how to say.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that usually happens right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed one specific thing that always happens right before. It’s usually when I’m on the trail or in my painting room, and then I hear Garrett’s voice so clearly it’s like he’s standing right behind me. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're reliving a memory of Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like a memory, no. It’s more like I’m hearing him right there in the present, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. I’ll be on the trail or in my painting room, and it feels completely real in that moment, even though part of me knows he’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett go away when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I can try to ignore it or remind myself Garrett is dead, but when I hear him it’s clear, like he’s right behind me. I don’t feel like I can just make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see Garrett or anything else that isn't really there when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see him, no. It’s mainly his voice—clear, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. In the moment it feels real, even though I know he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is a part of you or separate from you?\n\nPatient: It feels separate from me. It’s not like my own thoughts or an inner voice. I hear Garrett’s voice clearly, like he’s right behind me—on the trail or in my painting room—and in that moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes, I can control what I’m doing, but it gets really hard to stay grounded. When I hear Garrett’s voice, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in the moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that. Nothing specific comes to mind that I’d call traumatic, at least not in the way people usually mean. I’ve had hard things happen, but I don’t know if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s stressful because it sounds so real, like Garrett is right behind me. Even though I know he’s dead, when I hear it on the trail or in my painting room, it feels completely real in that moment, and that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt overwhelmed or kind of unreal at times, but I don’t remember feeling like I was actually outside my body watching myself.\n\nDoctor: Was Garrett's death a sudden or unexpected event for you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t have a clear answer for that. I know his death affects me, but whether it was sudden or unexpected… I don’t know how to say.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that usually happens right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed one specific thing that always happens right before. It’s usually when I’m on the trail or in my painting room, and then I hear Garrett’s voice so clearly it’s like he’s standing right behind me. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're reliving a memory of Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like a memory, no. It’s more like I’m hearing him right there in the present, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. I know part of me knows he’s gone, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a different state or not yourself when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like I’m in a different state, exactly. I still feel like me. But when I hear Garrett’s voice, it feels so real—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment, it doesn’t feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually hear Garrett's voice when you are feeling a certain way emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It wasn’t just normal sadness or missing him. I only started hearing Garrett during that wired period where I barely slept and felt like I had to keep going, taking on things that were way too much. Before that shift, I never heard him like that, even when I was grieving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're disconnected from yourself or the world around you when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I still feel like I’m there and I’m myself. It’s more that the voice feels real in the moment, but I don’t notice feeling detached from my body or like the world isn’t real.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing Garrett’s voice. It’s not vague or like a memory—it’s clear, like he’s right behind me, sometimes when I’m out on the trail or in my painting room. I know, at least part of me knows, that he’s dead, but when it happens it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I hear his voice clearly, like he’s right behind me. I’ll be on the trail or in my painting room, and it feels completely real in that moment, even though part of me knows he’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett go away when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I can try to ignore it or remind myself Garrett is dead, but when I hear him it’s clear, like he’s right behind me. I don’t feel like I can just make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see Garrett or anything else that isn't really there when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see him, no. It’s mainly his voice—clear, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. In the moment it feels real, even though I know he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is a part of you or separate from you?\n\nPatient: It feels separate from me. It’s not like my own thoughts or an inner voice. I hear Garrett’s voice clearly, like he’s right behind me—on the trail or in my painting room—and in that moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes, I can control what I’m doing, but it gets really hard to stay grounded. When I hear Garrett’s voice, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in the moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that. Nothing specific comes to mind that I’d call traumatic, at least not in the way people usually mean. I’ve had hard things happen, but I don’t know if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s stressful because it sounds so real, like Garrett is right behind me. Even though I know he’s dead, when I hear it on the trail or in my painting room, it feels completely real in that moment, and that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt overwhelmed or kind of unreal at times, but I don’t remember feeling like I was actually outside my body watching myself.\n\nDoctor: Was Garrett's death a sudden or unexpected event for you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t have a clear answer for that. I know his death affects me, but whether it was sudden or unexpected… I don’t know how to say.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that usually happens right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed one specific thing that always happens right before. It’s usually when I’m on the trail or in my painting room, and then I hear Garrett’s voice so clearly it’s like he’s standing right behind me. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're reliving a memory of Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like a memory, no. It’s more like I’m hearing him right there in the present, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. I know part of me knows he’s gone, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a different state or not yourself when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like I’m in a different state, exactly. times the thought of ending it has felt close enough that I told my wife I couldn’t trust myself alone.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F44.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing Garrett’s voice. It’s not vague or like a memory—it’s clear, like he’s right behind me, sometimes when I’m out on the trail or in my painting room. I know, at least part of me knows, that he’s dead, but when it happens it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I hear his voice clearly, like he’s right behind me. I’ll be on the trail or in my painting room, and it feels completely real in that moment, even though part of me knows he’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett go away when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I can try to ignore it or remind myself Garrett is dead, but when I hear him it’s clear, like he’s right behind me. I don’t feel like I can just make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see Garrett or anything else that isn't really there when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see him, no. It’s mainly his voice—clear, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. In the moment it feels real, even though I know he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is a part of you or separate from you?\n\nPatient: It feels separate from me. It’s not like my own thoughts or an inner voice. I hear Garrett’s voice clearly, like he’s right behind me—on the trail or in my painting room—and in that moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes, I can control what I’m doing, but it gets really hard to stay grounded. When I hear Garrett’s voice, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in the moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that. Nothing specific comes to mind that I’d call traumatic, at least not in the way people usually mean. I’ve had hard things happen, but I don’t know if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s stressful because it sounds so real, like Garrett is right behind me. Even though I know he’s dead, when I hear it on the trail or in my painting room, it feels completely real in that moment, and that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt overwhelmed or kind of unreal at times, but I don’t remember feeling like I was actually outside my body watching myself.\n\nDoctor: Was Garrett's death a sudden or unexpected event for you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t have a clear answer for that. I know his death affects me, but whether it was sudden or unexpected… I don’t know how to say.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that usually happens right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed one specific thing that always happens right before. It’s usually when I’m on the trail or in my painting room, and then I hear Garrett’s voice so clearly it’s like he’s standing right behind me. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're reliving a memory of Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like a memory, no. It’s more like I’m hearing him right there in the present, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. I know part of me knows he’s gone, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a different state or not yourself when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like I’m in a different state, exactly. I still feel like me. But when I hear Garrett’s voice, it feels so real—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment, it doesn’t feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually hear Garrett's voice when you are feeling a certain way emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It wasn’t just normal sadness or missing him. I only started hearing Garrett during that wired period where I barely slept and felt like I had to keep going, taking on things that were way too much. Before that shift, I never heard him like that, even when I was grieving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're disconnected from yourself or the world around you when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I still feel like I’m there and I’m myself. It’s more that the voice feels real in the moment, but I don’t notice feeling detached from my body or like the world isn’t real.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually hearing Garrett's voice when you're feeling overwhelmed or under a lot of stress?\n\nPatient: Not exactly ordinary stress, no. It started during that wired stretch, when I was barely sleeping and taking on these impossible projects and felt like I had to keep going. It’s not like I’m calling it up to comfort myself. I came in because I keep hearing Garrett’s voice like he’s right there—behind me on the trail, or in my painting room. Part of me knows he’s dead, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're hearing Garrett's voice as a way to escape or get away from something else that's going on in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s about escaping. It’s more like it just happens—Garrett’s voice is suddenly there, clear as if he’s standing behind me or in the room. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing Garrett’s voice. It’s not vague or like a memory—it’s clear, like he’s right behind me, sometimes when I’m out on the trail or in my painting room. I know, at least part of me knows, that he’s dead, but when it happens it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I hear his voice clearly, like he’s right behind me. I’ll be on the trail or in my painting room, and it feels completely real in that moment, even though part of me knows he’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett go away when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I can try to ignore it or remind myself Garrett is dead, but when I hear him it’s clear, like he’s right behind me. I don’t feel like I can just make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see Garrett or anything else that isn't really there when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see him, no. It’s mainly his voice—clear, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. In the moment it feels real, even though I know he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is a part of you or separate from you?\n\nPatient: It feels separate from me. It’s not like my own thoughts or an inner voice. I hear Garrett’s voice clearly, like he’s right behind me—on the trail or in my painting room—and in that moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes, I can control what I’m doing, but it gets really hard to stay grounded. When I hear Garrett’s voice, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in the moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that. Nothing specific comes to mind that I’d call traumatic, at least not in the way people usually mean. I’ve had hard things happen, but I don’t know if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s stressful because it sounds so real, like Garrett is right behind me. Even though I know he’s dead, when I hear it on the trail or in my painting room, it feels completely real in that moment, and that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt overwhelmed or kind of unreal at times, but I don’t remember feeling like I was actually outside my body watching myself.\n\nDoctor: Was Garrett's death a sudden or unexpected event for you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t have a clear answer for that. I know his death affects me, but whether it was sudden or unexpected… I don’t know how to say.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that usually happens right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed one specific thing that always happens right before. It’s usually when I’m on the trail or in my painting room, and then I hear Garrett’s voice so clearly it’s like he’s standing right behind me. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're reliving a memory of Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like a memory, no. It’s more like I’m hearing him right there in the present, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. I know part of me knows he’s gone, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a different state or not yourself when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like I’m in a different state, exactly. It started during that wired stretch, when I was barely sleeping and taking on these impossible projects and felt like I had to keep going. Before that mood shift, I never heard Garrett like that—not just from grieving or being upset.\n\nDoctor: Do you think hearing Garrett's voice is a way for you to cope with your emotions or difficult situations?\n\nPatient: I don’t really experience it as coping, no. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing Garrett’s voice. It’s not vague or like a memory—it’s clear, like he’s right behind me, sometimes when I’m out on the trail or in my painting room. I know, at least part of me knows, that he’s dead, but when it happens it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Does Garrett's voice ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: It’s more that I hear his voice clearly, like he’s right behind me. I’ll be on the trail or in my painting room, and it feels completely real in that moment, even though part of me knows he’s gone.\n\nDoctor: Can you make the voice of Garrett go away when you want it to?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I can try to ignore it or remind myself Garrett is dead, but when I hear him it’s clear, like he’s right behind me. I don’t feel like I can just make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever see Garrett or anything else that isn't really there when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see him, no. It’s mainly his voice—clear, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. In the moment it feels real, even though I know he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is a part of you or separate from you?\n\nPatient: It feels separate from me. It’s not like my own thoughts or an inner voice. I hear Garrett’s voice clearly, like he’s right behind me—on the trail or in my painting room—and in that moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control what you're doing when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Mostly, yes, I can control what I’m doing, but it gets really hard to stay grounded. When I hear Garrett’s voice, it’s so clear—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room—that in the moment it feels completely real, even though part of me knows he’s dead.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any traumatic events in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that. Nothing specific comes to mind that I’d call traumatic, at least not in the way people usually mean. I’ve had hard things happen, but I don’t know if that’s what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or stressed when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s stressful because it sounds so real, like Garrett is right behind me. Even though I know he’s dead, when I hear it on the trail or in my painting room, it feels completely real in that moment, and that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were outside of your body watching yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt overwhelmed or kind of unreal at times, but I don’t remember feeling like I was actually outside my body watching myself.\n\nDoctor: Was Garrett's death a sudden or unexpected event for you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t have a clear answer for that. I know his death affects me, but whether it was sudden or unexpected… I don’t know how to say.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that usually happens right before you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed one specific thing that always happens right before. It’s usually when I’m on the trail or in my painting room, and then I hear Garrett’s voice so clearly it’s like he’s standing right behind me. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're reliving a memory of Garrett when you hear his voice?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like a memory, no. It’s more like I’m hearing him right there in the present, like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. I know part of me knows he’s gone, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a different state or not yourself when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel like I’m in a different state, exactly. I still feel like me. But when I hear Garrett’s voice, it feels so real—like he’s right behind me on the trail or in my painting room. Part of me knows he’s dead, but in that moment, it doesn’t feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually hear Garrett's voice when you are feeling a certain way emotionally?\n\nPatient: Yeah. It wasn’t just normal sadness or missing him. I only started hearing Garrett during that wired period where I barely slept and felt like I had to keep going, taking on things that were way too much. Before that shift, I never heard him like that, even when I was grieving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're disconnected from yourself or the world around you when you hear Garrett's voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I still feel like I’m there and I’m myself. It’s more that the voice feels real in the moment, but I don’t notice feeling detached from my body or like the world isn’t real.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually hearing Garrett's voice when you're feeling overwhelmed or under a lot of stress?\n\nPatient: Not exactly ordinary stress, no. It started during that wired stretch, when I was barely sleeping and taking on these impossible projects and felt like I had to keep going. Before that mood shift, I never heard Garrett like that—not just from grieving or being upset.\n\nDoctor: Do you think hearing Garrett's voice is a way for you to cope with your emotions or difficult situations?\n\nPatient: I don’t really experience it as coping, no. It’s not like I’m calling it up to comfort myself. I came in because I keep hearing Garrett’s voice like he’s right there—behind me on the trail, or in my painting room. Part of me knows he’s dead, but when it happens, it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're hearing Garrett's voice as a way to escape or get away from something else that's going on in your life?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s about escaping. I know, at least part of me knows, that he’s dead, but in that moment it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is something that you are intentionally creating or is it something that happens to you without your control?\n\nPatient: It happens to me without my control. I’m not making it up on purpose or trying to hear him. It’s just suddenly there—Garrett’s voice, clear like he’s standing behind me on the trail or in my painting room. I know part of me knows he’s dead, but in the moment it feels completely real.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voice of Garrett is related to a specific part of yourself or a specific situation that triggers it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that it’s tied to a specific part of me. It seems more connected to certain places or moments—like when I’m on the trail or in my painting room. Then I hear Garrett’s voice very clearly, like he’s right behind me or in the room. Part of me knows he’s dead, but when it’s happening, it feels real.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
